The request carries identified `Message` values, `tools`, and\r\n  `options`; the response is NDJSON; the terminal frame carries the authoritative `ProviderResult`\r\n  so `thinking`, `tools`, and `usage` survive the hop; a server-side abort becomes an abort frame\r\n  the browser rethrows as `ProviderAbortError`.\r\n- `authorize` on the server half is mandatory; token minting, validation, and identity stay in the\r\n  application. The browser half authenticates through the existing `headers` hook.\r\n- `buildResult` moves to agent; `OllamaResponse` is retired; ollama's `joinThinking` is deleted in\r\n  favour of the agent's (probed: the two functions have different signatures and disagree on\r\n  empty input).\r\n- Publish agent `0.0.22` first, then ollama `0.0.16` re-pinned to `^0.0.22`; toolbox re-pins in\r\n  its own later campaign. During the campaign the agent artifact reaches ollama as an installed\r\n  packed tarball, never a link.\r\n- No browser Vitest project and no new dependency.\r\n\r\n## Rulings on the disagreements\r\n\r\n| # | Question | Subjective lane | Objective lane | Ruling and reason |\r\n| - | -------- | --------------- | -------------- | ----------------- |\r\n| 1 | Seam form | Abstract methods on the subclass (`body`, `read`) plus a public `request`; `url`, `path`, `frame` as constructor input | A composed `wire` object exposed as a readonly property, with `finish`, `error`, `unary`, `split`, `strict` | **Abstract methods on the subclass, with the objective lane's seam set.** The subclass fills `frame`, `body`, `read`, and `finish`; `url`, `path`, `split`, and `strict` are constructor input; `name` is an abstract data member. A wire object would leave `OllamaProvider` an empty subclass, which the wrapper test forbids. `request` stays `#`-private: nothing outside the base needs it once `generate` is ruled below. The objective lane was right that end-of-input is wire-specific (`finish`), and that the relay must not re-split (`split`) and must require a terminal frame (`strict`). |\r\n| 2 | `generate` | Drains `stream`; Ollama overrides with its native `stream: false` call | Base selects a native unary path when the wire supplies a decoder | **`generate` drains `stream`, and nobody overrides it.** One engine, exactly as `Agent.generate()` drains `Agent.stream()`. Ollama's `stream: false` mode is not a faster path, only a second flow; its guide clause and the request-shape assertion that pin `stream: false` are ours to change and are recorded as a release change. This also removes the multi-line-JSON hazard that framing a unary body through an NDJSON parser would have introduced. |\r\n| 3 | HTTP error | Shared `ProviderHTTPError` in agent; ollama's error class removed (flagged as the user's call) | Wire-specific `error(failure)` translation keeping `OllamaHTTPError` | **One shared `ProviderError` in agent with `code: 'HTTP' \\| 'PROTOCOL' \\| 'LIMIT' \\| 'PROVIDER'` and an optional `status`; `OllamaHTTPError`, `isOllamaHTTPError`, and `OllamaHTTPErrorOptions` are removed.** The only vendor-specific content of ollama's error is the word \"Ollama\" in its message. A per-vendor error seam would be a wrapper. Recorded for the user as a public removal they can reverse. |\r\n| 4 | Error-body bound | Slice after `response.text()` (as today) | Stop reading after a bounded prefix and cancel the remainder | **Objective lane.** Reproduced: `OllamaProvider.ts:370` reads the whole error body before slicing, which does not bound the read. The base reads at most `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH` and cancels the rest. |\r\n| 5 | Body readers | Inline reader loop in the base | A `ProviderReader` class with limits and truncation shared by base and relay | **Two exported helpers rather than a class:** `readText(body, limit?)` collects a bounded text and cancels the remainder, and `readChunks(body)` yields decoded text with a final flush and a cancel in `finally`. Both are pure leaves over a `ReadableStream`; the relay handler reuses `readText` to bound its request body. |\r\n| 6 | Relay frame vocabulary | `channel` discriminant reusing `ProviderDelta` as-is; `settle`, `abort`, `error` arms | `event` discriminant with a `sequence` number and a `version: 1` request field | **`channel`, reusing `ProviderDelta` verbatim; arms `result`, `abort`, `error`; no `sequence`, no `version`.** One discriminant name for one concept. Loss detection buys little because the `result` frame is authoritative for the final value and `strict` already fails end-of-input without it; a version field on a `0.0.x` greenfield wire is speculation. |\r\n| 7 | `authorize` return | `boolean`; false answers `401` | `Response \\| undefined`; the application supplies the denial | **`boolean`.** Anything richer composes in front as ordinary `@orkestrel/server` middleware, which is the line's documented pattern for MCP. |\r\n| 8 | Request body bound on the server | Not addressed | Finite configurable limits | **Adopted for the request:** `RelayOptions.limit` in bytes, default `1_048_576`, answered with `413`. Provider success bodies stay unbounded as today and the guide says so. |\r\n| 9 | JSON representability across the hop | Not addressed | Reject unrepresentable values before dispatch; do not silently drop `caller` | **Adopted.** `RelayProvider.body` projects the request through a `providerRequestToJSON` helper gated by contract's JSON guards, exactly as `agentResultToJSON` already does, and throws `ProviderError('PROTOCOL')` when the value is not JSON. `ToolCall.caller` never crosses the hop and the guide states it (it is declared `unknown`, consumer-asserted context). |\r\n| 10 | `isMessage` | Not addressed | The guard is weaker than the type; fix it, do not add a second guard | **Adopted as a repair with red/green.** Reproduced at `validators.ts:36-42`: any `role` string and any `images` array pass. The guard now narrows to the `MessageRole` union and string image elements. A stored snapshot carrying a role outside the union now reads as absent, which is the fail-closed reading the guard already claims for `calls`. |\r\n| 11 | Placement | `providers/AgentProvider.ts` | `AgentProvider.ts` at the module root | **Root for the defining base, `providers/` for concrete providers:** `agent/src/core/AgentProvider.ts`, `agent/src/core/providers/RelayProvider.ts`, `agent/src/core/RelayStream.ts`; `ollama/src/core/OllamaProvider.ts` stays where its class sits today. |\r\n| 12 | Browser proof | Core-scoped typecheck plus a bound-`fetch` runtime assertion; state the limit | The same, plus a real Chromium smoke through the available harness | **Both.** C3 closes on the mechanical evidence and on a Chromium receipt the Orchestrator takes with the harness browser against the built exports, retained under `.orkestrel/campaign/`. No persistent browser project. |\r\n| 13 | Scope list omissions | �?\" | `guides/README.md` and the lockfiles are outside the may-change list | **Adopted.** Both packages' `guides/README.md` and ollama's `package-lock.json` join the scope. |\r\n| 14 | Unresolved `headers` hook under abort | Not addressed | A timeout signal handed to `fetch` cannot interrupt an unresolved hook promise | **Adopted.** The base races the hook against the combined signal and proves it with a never-resolving hook. |\r\n| 15 | `usage` gating | Fold whatever `read` reports | A usage frame replaces the prior report | **Same behaviour, adopted as `ProviderIncrement.usage?`:** present replaces, absent keeps. Ollama's `extractUsage` already returns `undefined` unless both counts are present, so its observable behaviour is unchanged. |\r\n\r\n## Which lane was right on what\r\n\r\n- The subjective lane was right on the seam form, the discriminant, the `authorize` shape, the\r\n  name set, the `joinThinking` collision, and the unit decomposition.\r\n- The objective lane was right on `finish`, `split`, `strict`, the unbounded error read, the\r\n  request-body bound, JSON representability, the `isMessage` defect, the unresolved-hook hazard,\r\n  the scope omissions, and the Chromium receipt.\r\n- The Orchestrator overruled both on `generate` and on the error taxonomy, for the reasons in the\r\n  table.\r\n\r\n## Decisions made on the user's behalf, reversible\r\n\r\n1. `OllamaHTTPError`, `isOllamaHTTPError`, `OllamaHTTPErrorOptions`, `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH`,\r\n   `DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`, `buildResult`, `joinThinking`, `parseBody`, and `OllamaResponse`\r\n   leave `@orkestrel/ollama`; the first five reappear in `@orkestrel/agent` as `ProviderError`,\r\n   `isProviderError`, `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH`, `DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`, and\r\n   `buildProviderResult`.\r\n2. `OllamaProvider.generate` no longer sends `stream: false`; it drains the stream.\r\n3. `@orkestrel/ollama` publishes a core face instead of a server face.\r\n4. `@orkestrel/agent` gains no runtime dependency; a relay browser app passes\r\n   `createNDJSONParser` from `@orkestrel/ndjson` to `RelayProvider` itself.\r\n# Plan �?\" provider base, `OllamaProvider` on it, browser�+\"server relay\r\n\r\nStatus: design reconciled 2026-09-14 (see `design-reconciliation.md`); implementation units\r\ndispatching. `registry.md` holds the routing ledger and bench state. This file is rewritten at each\r\nre-baseline.\r\n\r\n## Enumerated scope (fixed 2026-09-14 at campaign start)\r\n\r\n| Capability | Closes when                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | State |\r\n| ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----- |\r\n| C1         | `@orkestrel/agent` core exports a host-independent provider base that owns every provider mechanic up to the wire-specific seams, typed in `types.ts`, barrelled, tested in `src:core`, and documented in `agent.md`             | open  |\r\n| C2         | `OllamaProvider` is rebuilt on that base with no duplicated mechanic; ollama's unit, conformance, and service suites are green                                                                                                     | open  |\r\n| C3         | A browser runtime can import and run the provider: host-independence proven under the core scope with no Node types, a bound-`fetch` runtime assertion, and a Chromium receipt driving the built exports                          | open  |\r\n| C4         | A browser-side provider relays to a server-side provider through the developer's server: custom token in, real credential server-side only, deltas streamed back, abort crossing the hop, policy hooks left to the application | open  |\r\n| C5         | Guides, parity, tests, and the gate chain green in agent and ollama; publish order agent then ollama prepared                                                                                                                      | open  |\r\n\r\n## Exit criterion\r\n\r\nEvery row above ends implemented, repaired, retained, or intentionally excluded on evidence, and\r\n`npm run format:check`, `lint:check`, `check`, `build`, and `test` are green in agent and ollama\r\nas run by an independent `verifier`, with ollama's `test:service` green against the warm daemon.\r\nPublishing is the user's decision and outside the criterion.\r\n\r\n## The ruled contract\r\n\r\nAdditions to `agent/src/core/types.ts` (TSDoc omitted here; the unit writes it):\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nexport interface ProviderParserInterface<TRecord = Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>> {\r\n\tparse(chunk: string): readonly TRecord[]\r\n\tclear(): void\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface ProviderRequest {\r\n\treadonly messages: readonly Message[]\r\n\treadonly tools?: readonly ToolDefinition[]\r\n\treadonly options?: ProviderStreamOptions\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface ProviderIncrement {\r\n\treadonly content: string\r\n\treadonly thinking: string\r\n\treadonly tools: readonly ToolCall[]\r\n\treadonly usage?: TokenUsage\r\n\treadonly result?: ProviderResult\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface ProviderOptions {\r\n\treadonly timeout?: number\r\n\treadonly fetch?: typeof globalThis.fetch\r\n\treadonly headers?: () =>\r\n\t\t| Readonly<Record<string, string>>\r\n\t\t| Promise<Readonly<Record<string, string>>>\r\n\treadonly format?: ContextFormat\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface AgentProviderInput extends ProviderOptions {\r\n\treadonly url: string\r\n\treadonly path?: string\r\n\treadonly split?: boolean\r\n\treadonly strict?: boolean\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface AgentProviderInterface<TRecord = Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>>\r\n\textends ProviderInterface {\r\n\tframe(): ProviderParserInterface<TRecord>\r\n\tbody(request: ProviderRequest): object\r\n\tread(record: TRecord): ProviderIncrement\r\n\tfinish(parser: ProviderParserInterface<TRecord>): readonly TRecord[]\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport type ProviderErrorCode = 'HTTP' | 'PROTOCOL' | 'LIMIT' | 'PROVIDER'\r\n\r\nexport interface ProviderErrorOptions {\r\n\treadonly status?: number\r\n\treadonly cause?: unknown\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport type RelayFrame =\r\n\t| ProviderDelta\r\n\t| { readonly channel: 'result'; readonly result: ProviderResult }\r\n\t| { readonly channel: 'abort'; readonly partial: ProviderResult }\r\n\t| { readonly channel: 'error'; readonly code: 'PROVIDER'; readonly message: string }\r\n\r\nexport type RelayHandler = (request: Request) => Promise<Response>\r\n\r\nexport interface RelayOptions {\r\n\treadonly provider: ProviderInterface\r\n\treadonly authorize: (request: Request) => boolean | Promise<boolean>\r\n\treadonly limit?: number\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface RelayStreamOptions {\r\n\treadonly provider: ProviderInterface\r\n\treadonly request: ProviderRequest\r\n\treadonly signal: AbortSignal\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport interface RelayProviderOptions extends ProviderOptions {\r\n\treadonly url: string\r\n\treadonly frame: () => ProviderParserInterface\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\nSemantics the base owns: a fresh `crypto.randomUUID()` `id`; `format` exposed as given; per-call\r\n`Timeout` plus `AbortSignal.any([timeout.signal, signal])`; the bound default `fetch` and the\r\nawaited `headers` hook raced against the combined signal, merged over `Content-Type: application/json`;\r\none POST of `JSON.stringify(body(request))` to `url + (path ?? '')`; a non-OK status read to at\r\nmost `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH` then cancelled and thrown as `ProviderError('HTTP', message, { status })`;\r\na `null` body thrown as `ProviderError('PROTOCOL')`; the response body decoded through `readChunks`\r\nand framed by one `frame()` parser per call; each record through `read`; when `split` (default\r\n`true`) raw `content` goes through one `createThinkSplitter()` per call and clean content is yielded\r\nas `{ channel: 'content' }`; native `thinking` yielded as `{ channel: 'thinking' }`; `tools`\r\nappended; a present `usage` replaces the running one; a present `result` becomes the authoritative\r\nsettled result; at end of input `finish(parser)` is fed through `read` too, the splitter is flushed,\r\nand when `strict` and no `result` arrived a `ProviderError('PROTOCOL')` is thrown; a cancel of the\r\ncombined signal throws `ProviderAbortError` carrying the partial assembled so far; any other error\r\n(a `ProviderAbortError` thrown by `read` for a remote abort included) propagates unchanged; the\r\nreader is cancelled, the parser cleared, and the timeout cleared in `finally`; `generate` drains\r\n`stream` with explicit `next()` calls and returns the terminal value. Both lanes' evidence for each\r\nof these is in `absorb-provider-report.md` item 4 and the two design reports.\r\n\r\nErrors in `agent/src/core/errors.ts`: `ProviderError` (`code: ProviderErrorCode`,\r\n`status: number | undefined`, `cause` through `ErrorOptions`) and `isProviderError`.\r\n\r\nConstants in `agent/src/core/constants.ts`: `DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT = 120_000`,\r\n`MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH = 2048`, `DEFAULT_RELAY_LIMIT = 1_048_576`, and the relay content type.\r\n\r\nHelpers in `agent/src/core/helpers.ts`: `buildProviderResult(content, thinking, tools, usage)`\r\n(from ollama's `buildResult`), `joinThinking` widened so an empty string on either side is treated\r\nas absent, `readText(body, limit?)`, `readChunks(body)`, `providerRequestToJSON(request)`.\r\n\r\nValidators in `agent/src/core/validators.ts`: `isMessage` repaired to the `MessageRole` union and\r\nstring image elements; `isProviderRequest`; `isRelayFrame`.\r\n\r\nFactories in `agent/src/core/factories.ts`: `createRelay(options): RelayHandler`,\r\n`createRelayProvider(options): RelayProvider`.\r\n\r\nClasses: `agent/src/core/AgentProvider.ts` (abstract), `agent/src/core/providers/RelayProvider.ts`\r\n(`name = 'relay'`, `split: false`, `strict: true`, `body` through `providerRequestToJSON`, `read`\r\nnarrows a record with `isRelayFrame`, maps deltas to increments, `result` to the settled result,\r\n`abort` to a thrown `ProviderAbortError(partial)`, `error` to a thrown `ProviderError('PROVIDER')`,\r\nand a bad frame to `ProviderError('PROTOCOL')`; `finish` returns `parser.parse('\\n')`),\r\n`agent/src/core/RelayStream.ts` (a pull-driven `Response` over one `provider.stream` call: one\r\nawaited `next()` per pull, frames as `JSON.stringify(frame) + '\\n'`, a `result` frame on completion,\r\nan `abort` frame for a `ProviderAbortError`, an `error` frame with a fixed message for any other\r\nthrow, the upstream signal aborted then `iterator.return()` on body cancel, listeners released).\r\n\r\n`createRelay` flow: `authorize(request)` false �+' `401`; `readText(request.body, limit)` over the\r\nlimit �+' `413`; JSON parse plus `isProviderRequest` failure �+' `400`; otherwise\r\n`new RelayStream({ provider, request, signal: request.signal }).response` with\r\n`content-type: application/x-ndjson; charset=utf-8` and `cache-control: no-store`.\r\n\r\nOllama after the rebuild: `OllamaProvider extends AgentProvider` with `name = 'ollama'`,\r\n`super({ url: options.url ?? DEFAULT_OLLAMA_URL, path: OLLAMA_CHAT_PATH, ...options })`,\r\n`frame()` returning `createNDJSONParser()`, `body(request)` returning the `WireChatRequest` with\r\n`stream: true`, `read(record)` through `extractContent` / `extractThinking` / `extractTools` /\r\n`extractUsage`, `finish(parser)` returning `parser.parse('\\n')`. `OllamaOptions extends ProviderOptions`\r\nkeeps `model`, `url?`, `keepAlive?`, `options?`, `think?`. Removed from ollama: `OllamaResponse`,\r\n`OllamaHTTPError`, `isOllamaHTTPError`, `OllamaHTTPErrorOptions`, `MAX_ERROR_BODY_LENGTH`,\r\n`DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`, `buildResult`, `joinThinking`, `parseBody`; `errors.ts` and\r\n`parsers.ts` are deleted when empty. Added: `OLLAMA_CHAT_PATH`.\r\n\r\n## Units and routing ledger\r\n# Unit A1 (successor) �?\" `AgentProvider`: the host-independent provider base in `@orkestrel/agent` core\r\n\r\nThis brief supersedes `tmp/units/a1-brief.md` after its first run stopped on a deviation. Apply\r\nevery section of that file exactly as written, except the sections restated here, which replace\r\ntheir originals. Read that file first, then this one.\r\n\r\n## What changed and why\r\n\r\nThe first run stopped correctly: `tmp/units/a1-report.md` shows that the original A\u0015 \"Shapes and\r\ncontracts\" required each wire shape's `Infer` to **equal** its domain type, while the domain types\r\nadmit non-JSON values �?\" `ToolCall.arguments: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>`\r\n(`node_modules/@orkestrel/tool/dist/src/core/index.d.ts:126`), `ToolDefinition.parameters`\r\n(`:143`), and `ProviderStreamOptions.schema` (`src/core/types.ts:121`) �?\" so a JSON-only shape can\r\nonly be narrower. The Orchestrator rules: **the wire shapes are JSON projections of the domain\r\ntypes, strictly narrower by design, and the proof is assignability, not equality.** Nothing else in\r\nthe original brief changes. The `prove` MCP tool is unavailable inside this exec (its approval\r\npolicy blocks it, as the report records); use `expectTypeOf` assertions in the test files, which\r\nthe root `npm run check` typechecks.\r\n\r\n## Shapes and contracts (replaces the original section)\r\n\r\nDeclare one `ContractShape` per wire body in `src/core/shapers.ts` with the `*Shape` form the\r\ncontract guide fixes, and compile each once with `createContract` in `src/core/contracts.ts`:\r\n\r\n- a message shape: `id` string, `role` as `literalShape` over the `MessageRole` union, `content`\r\n  string, optional `calls` as an array of `{ id: string, name: string, arguments: recordShape(jsonShape()) }`,\r\n  optional `images` as an array of strings; `ToolCall.caller` is not in the shape and never\r\n  crosses a wire �?\" say so in the shape's TSDoc;\r\n- a provider-request shape: `messages` (the message shape), optional `tools` as an array of\r\n  `{ name: string, description?: string, parameters?: recordShape(jsonShape()) }`, optional\r\n  `options` as `{ think?: boolean, schema?: recordShape(jsonShape()) }`;\r\n- a provider-result shape: `content` string, optional `thinking` string, optional `tools` (the\r\n  tool-call shape), optional `usage` as `{ prompt, completion, total }` numbers;\r\n- a relay-frame shape: a `unionShape` over the four `RelayFrame` arms discriminated on `channel`.\r\n\r\nProve, in `tests/src/core/shapers.test.ts` with `expectTypeOf`, that each shape's `Infer` is\r\n**assignable to** its domain type (`toMatchTypeOf` / `toExtend`, whichever the installed Vitest\r\nexposes): `Infer<typeof messageShape>` to `Message`, the request shape to `ProviderRequest`, the\r\nresult shape to `ProviderResult`, the frame shape to `RelayFrame`. State in the shape's TSDoc that\r\nthe wire is strictly narrower than the domain type: a domain value carrying a non-JSON\r\n`arguments`, `parameters`, or `schema` member, or a `caller`, is a valid domain value that the\r\nwire refuses or drops.\r\n\r\nProve the round trip in `tests/src/core/contracts.test.ts`: `contract.is(value)` on a valid value;\r\n`parseJSONAs(JSON.stringify(value), contract.is)` returning a deep-equal value;\r\n`contract.explain(malformed)` naming the path of a malformed field; and the asymmetry �?\" a message\r\nwhose `calls[0].arguments` holds a function value is refused by the compiled message contract.\r\n\r\nRepair `isMessage` (`src/core/validators.ts`) as the **domain** guard: `role` narrows to the\r\n`MessageRole` union and `images` elements must be strings, with a failing test first for the\r\narbitrary-role and non-string-image cases. `isMessage` keeps admitting a non-JSON `arguments`\r\nrecord, because the domain type does; the compiled message contract is the **wire** guard. Prove\r\nthe relation between them: every fixture the wire contract accepts is accepted by `isMessage`, and\r\nthe function-valued-`arguments` fixture is accepted by `isMessage` and refused by the wire contract.\r\n\r\n## Acceptance criteria (replaces items 3 and 5 of the original; the rest stand)\r\n\r\n3. Every declaration in A\u0015 Contract exists in `src/core/types.ts` with the stated members and is\r\n   exported from the barrel; `AgentProvider`, `ProviderError`, `isProviderError`,\r\n   `buildProviderResult`, `readText`, `readChunks`, the shapes, and the compiled contracts are\r\n   exported from the barrel; each shape's `Infer` is proven assignable to its domain type by an\r\n   `expectTypeOf` assertion that `npm run check` typechecks.\r\n5. `isMessage` rejects `{ id: '1', role: 'other', content: '' }` and\r\n   `{ id: '1', role: 'user', content: '', images: [1] }` and accepts a message whose\r\n   `calls[0].arguments` holds a function value; the compiled message contract refuses that last\r\n   fixture; red-then-green recorded for the two rejections.\r\n\r\n## Output (replaces the original path)\r\n\r\nWrite the report to `tmp/units/a1-report-2.md` and return the same text as your final message.\r\nLeave `tmp/units/a1-report.md` untouched; it is the record of the first run.\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Expiry sets `expired` and\r\n  * aborts the current signal once. `clear()` cancels without aborting and resets\r\n  * `expired`. Starting or clearing after expiry installs a fresh signal. A parent\r\n  * abort deliberately clears the timeout without aborting its signal; once the\r\n  * parent has aborted, later starts are inert.\r\n  *\r\n  * Construction validates the complete JavaScript boundary before creating an\r\n  * `AbortController`. A malformed or unreadable options record throws a\r\n  * `bound`-coded {@link import('@orkestrel/contract').ContractError}; an invalid\r\n  * `id`, `ms`, or `signal` uses `literal`, `range`, or `placement`, respectively.\r\n  *\r\n  * @example\r\n  * ```ts\r\n  * import { Timeout } from '@orkestrel/timeout'\r\n  *\r\n  * const timeout = new Timeout({ ms: 5_000 })\r\n  * timeout.start()\r\n  * timeout.signal.addEventListener('abort', () => giveUp(), { once: true })\r\n  * timeout.clear()\r\n  * ```\r\n  */\r\n export declare class Timeout implements TimeoutInterface {\r\n     #private;\r\n     constructor(options: TimeoutOptions);\r\n     get id(): string;\r\n     get ms(): number;\r\n     get signal(): AbortSignal;\r\n     get expired(): boolean;\r\n     start(): void;\r\n     clear(): void;\r\n }\r\n\r\n /**\r\n  * Represents a controllable deadline exposing a native `AbortSignal` that aborts on expiry.\r\n  *\r\n  * @example\r\n  * ```ts\r\n  * const timeout: TimeoutInterface = createTimeout({ ms: 5_000 })\r\n  * timeout.start()\r\n  * timeout.clear()\r\n  * ```\r\n  */\r\n export declare interface TimeoutInterface {\r\n     /** Holds the trace label supplied at construction or generated as a random UUID. */\r\n     readonly id: string;\r\n     /** Holds the validated integer deadline in milliseconds. */\r\n     readonly ms: number;\r\n     /**\r\n      * Holds the native signal that aborts once when the current deadline\r\n      * expires. A `start()` or `clear()` call after an expiry installs a fresh\r\n      * signal, so a caller holding an earlier reference keeps the aborted one.\r\n      */\r\n     readonly signal: AbortSignal;\r\n     /** Reports whether the owned signal has aborted, derived directly from that signal. */\r\n     readonly expired: boolean;\r\n     /**\r\n      * Arms or re-arms the deadline for `ms`, installing a fresh `signal` when the\r\n      * current one has already aborted.\r\n      *\r\n      * @remarks\r\n      * Returns without arming when the parent `signal` supplied at construction\r\n      * has already aborted, so a handle whose parent aborted never expires again\r\n      * and its `signal` never fires.\r\n      *\r\n      * @returns Nothing\r\n      */\r\n     start(): void;\r\n     /**\r\n      * Cancels an armed deadline without aborting its `signal`, and resets expiry by\r\n      * installing a fresh signal when the current one has already aborted.\r\n      *\r\n      * @remarks\r\n      * A signal that never fired keeps its identity.\r\n      *\r\n      * @returns Nothing\r\n      */\r\n     clear(): void;\r\n }\r\n\r\n /**\r\n  * Represents the options `createTimeout` and the `Timeout` constructor accept.\r\n  *\r\n  * @remarks\r\n  * `ms` is an integer from `0` through `2_147_483_647`, inclusive. `id`\r\n  * defaults to a random UUID when omitted, and `signal` is an optional native\r\n  * parent signal whose abort clears an armed timeout without aborting the\r\n  * timeout's own signal.\r\n  *\r\n  * @example\r\n  * ```ts\r\n  * const options: TimeoutOptions = { id: 'request', ms: 5_000 }\r\n  * ```\r\n  */\r\n export declare interface TimeoutOptions {\r\n     /** Holds the trace label for the timeout; omission generates a random UUID. */\r\n     readonly id?: string;\r\n     /** Holds the integer deadline in milliseconds, inclusive from `0` through `2_147_483_647`. */\r\n     readonly ms: number;\r\n     /** Holds the native parent signal whose abort clears an armed timeout. */\r\n     readonly signal?: AbortSignal;\r\n }\r\n\r\n /**\r\n  * Validates once-read timeout construction options and returns a fresh normalized copy\r\n  * omitting absent optional keys.\r\n  *\r\n  * @remarks\r\n  * Each property is read before any validation runs, and no timer, controller, or\r\n  * listener lifecycle begins at this boundary.\r\n  *\r\n  * @param options - Potentially untrusted timeout options\r\n  * @returns A fresh validated `TimeoutOptions` object\r\n  * @throws {@link import('@orkestrel/contract').ContractError} Thrown when the\r\n      *   input does not satisfy `TimeoutOptions`\r\n      *\r\n      * @example\r\n      * ```ts\r\n      * const options = validateTimeoutOptions({ ms: 5_000 })\r\n      * ```\r\n      */\r\n  export declare function validateTimeoutOptions(options: TimeoutOptions): TimeoutOptions;\r\n\r\n  export { }\r\n# Guides\r\n\r\nA dual-axis index into this repository's guides �?\" by concept, and by directory.\r\n\r\n## By concept\r\n\r\n| Concept | Spec                   | Source                    | Tests                                 |\r\n| ------- | ---------------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------- |\r\n| Agent   | [`agent.md`](agent.md) | [`src/core`](../src/core) | [`tests/src/core`](../tests/src/core) |\r\n\r\n## By directory\r\n\r\n| Directory  | Guide                  |\r\n| ---------- | ---------------------- |\r\n| `src/core` | [`agent.md`](agent.md) |\r\n\r\n## Dependency reference\r\n\r\n[`abort.md`](abort.md) is a byte-identical mirror of the guide for\r\n`@orkestrel/abort` �?\" a runtime dependency, the cancellation primitive an\r\nagent turn's `signal` is folded from. Reasoning separation, the authority gate, and durable jobs sit around that boundary. The model itself is the one thing this package does not supply: `ProviderInterface` is a contract, not an implementation, so any backend that satisfies it drops in unchanged and the host application decides which one. Nor is there hidden global state, a plugin lifecycle, a prompt-template DSL, or an implicit memory store. This is a kit of composable primitives: the loop is the convenient way to use them, not the only one, and a caller that would rather bound and drive a provider by hand can skip it entirely.\n16:## Surface\n18:The agent-owned surface: the inference boundary, the conversation layer, the context and its managers, the loop, the authority gate, and the durable-job bridge. Tool and workspace entities belong to their originating packages and are consumed directly — never re-exported here — and the concrete `ProviderInterface` implementation belongs to the host application.\n20:A provider turns a conversation (plus optional tools) into a turn: `generate` resolves the assembled `ProviderResult` (content + any tool calls + any usage); `stream` yields channel-tagged `ProviderDelta`s as they arrive (`content` for answer text, `thinking` for live reasoning) and returns the same assembled result when the stream completes, so a caller can render tokens / reasoning live and still get the full outcome. Both bound the call with an `AbortSignal`:\n23:import { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\n24:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n26:declare const provider: ProviderInterface // any concrete implementation supplied by the host app\n27:const abort = createAbort()\n30:const result = await provider.generate(messages, abort.signal)\n34:const generator = provider.stream(messages, abort.signal)\n38:\tif (step.value.channel === 'thinking') process.stderr.write(step.value.text)\n75:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n76:import { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\n79:declare const provider: ProviderInterface\n80:const abort = createAbort()\n89:const result = await provider.generate(input, abort.signal, definitions)\n98:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n102:declare const provider: ProviderInterface\n124:Both `generate` and `stream` accept optional per-run `AgentRunOptions` — `think` and `schema` (forwarded to the provider as `ProviderStreamOptions`) plus `limit` / `timeout` / `budget` / `signal`, each overriding its `AgentOptions` construction default for this run only. Omitting one keeps the constructed default, so a caller that passes no options gets the agent it configured. A per-run `signal` composes with (never replaces) a constructed `signal` — either aborting cancels the run; a per-run `budget` is `start()`ed for that run and is the one the loop charges, leaving a constructed `budget` untouched:\n140:The turn is bounded by one cancel folded from the external `signal` + the `timeout` deadline + the `budget` signal through `AbortSignal.any`; `agent.abort(reason)` (or `stream.abort(reason)`) fires it. A cancel — external, deadline, budget, or `abort()` — commits a partial result: the `result` promise resolves with `{ partial: true, content: <what accumulated> }`, never rejects (a cancel is not an error); only a genuine provider / tool error rejects. An optional `scheduler.yield`s between turns; tool iteration is capped at `limit` (default `DEFAULT_AGENT_LIMIT`). Exhausting `limit` while the model still holds unresolved tool intent (it requested tools on the very last allowed turn) is a distinct, non-cancel cause of `partial: true` — it fires an `exhaust` event (the turns reached) instead of `abort`. A natural finish on the last allowed turn, or `limit: 0` (which never enters the loop), stays `partial: false`. `agent.status` transitions `idle` → `running` → `done` / `error`.\n188:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n190:declare const provider: ProviderInterface // a vision-capable model\n193:\tabort.signal,\n213:### Conversations & compaction\n219:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n221:declare const provider: ProviderInterface // any concrete implementation supplied by the host app\n265:\t// + continues on the rebuilt (smaller) view (compact-and-continue), never aborts.\n272:##### One agent, many conversations (switching the active conversation)\n301:##### Production behaviors of automatic compaction\n315:### Scoping a turn\n340:### Customizing the format (the cascade)\n346:3. **Provider default** — `format?: ContextFormat` on a `ProviderInterface` (keyed by section kind — the `instructions` section): the model's preferred framing.                                               |\n588:## Methods\n590:The tables list every public call-signature member of `ProviderInterface`, `ThinkSplitterInterface`, `MessageManagerInterface`, `InstructionManagerInterface`, `ContextSectionSourceInterface`, `ScopeInterface`, `ScopeManagerInterface`, `AgentContextInterface`, `AgentInterface`, `StreamInterface`, `ChannelInterface`, `AuthorityInterface`, `AgentRegistryInterface`, `ConversationInterface`, `ConversationManagerInterface`, `ConversationStoreInterface`, `MemoryConversationStore`, and `DatabaseConversationStore`. Their readonly data members remain Surface rows. `ThinkSplitter`, `InstructionManager`, `Scope`, `ScopeManager`, `AgentContext`, `Agent`, `Authority`, `AgentRegistry`, `Conversation`, and `ConversationManager` implement their interfaces exactly, so the tables also describe those classes' instance methods. A host application supplies the concrete `ProviderInterface`. Tool and workspace methods live in their dependency guides.\n592:#### `ProviderInterface`\n599:| `stream`   | `AsyncGenerator<ProviderDelta, ProviderResult>` | Streams one turn — yields channel-tagged `content` / `thinking` `ProviderDelta`s as they arrive and returns the assembled `ProviderResult` (the concatenated content, any separated reasoning, any tool calls, and any usage) when the stream completes. A mid-stream abort throws a `ProviderAbortError` carrying the partial result. |\n601:#### `ThinkSplitterInterface`\n603:The stream-stateful `<think>…</think>` separator a provider routes raw content deltas through, so it yields clean content and surfaces the reasoning as `ProviderResult.thinking`. The `content` / `thinking` data members (the authoritative clean-content + reasoning accumulations) stay Surface rows — `content` matters because some chat templates pre-seed `<think>` into the prompt scaffold (the qwen3 shape), so only a bare `</think>` ever appears on the wire: before any tag event, that bare close reclassifies everything surfaced so far into `thinking` (one-shot — afterwards a bare close is plain text), correcting `content` retroactively where the already-returned deltas cannot be recalled. One splitter serves one stream — create a fresh one per call (`createThinkSplitter`).\n607:| `split` | `string` | Feeds one raw delta and returns the clean, non-think content to surface for it (possibly `''`) — a tag split across deltas is held until disambiguated, never leaked as content and never mis-eaten as thinking. |\n608:| `flush` | `string` | Settles the stream end — a held partial tag that never completed returns as the final content delta, and an unclosed think span's tail lands on `thinking`.                                                      |\n610:#### `MessageManagerInterface`\n622:#### `InstructionManagerInterface`\n635:#### `ContextSectionSourceInterface`\n643:#### `ScopeInterface`\n651:#### `ScopeManagerInterface`\n663:#### `AgentContextInterface`\n672:#### `AgentInterface`\n674:The bounded agent loop. `generate` and `stream` share one private run (`generate` drains the same stream `stream` exposes, so they can't diverge); `abort` cancels the in-flight turn. The `emitter` / `id` / `status` / `context` data members stay Surface rows (`emitter` is a `readonly` accessor — a property, not a method).\n680:| `abort`    | `void`                 | Cancels the in-flight turn — fires the turn's signal; the `result` settles `partial: true` with whatever content accumulated.                                                         |\n682:#### `StreamInterface`\n688:| `abort` | `void`  | Cancels the in-flight operation — fires its bound signal. |\n690:#### `ChannelInterface`\n718:#### `AuthorityInterface`\n726:#### `AgentRegistryInterface`\n732:| `provider`  | `ProviderInterface`  | Resolves a registered `ProviderInterface` by name — throws `unknown provider: <name>` when absent.                                                                                                                    |\n738:#### `ConversationInterface`\n756:#### `ConversationManagerInterface`\n771:#### `ConversationStoreInterface`\n781:#### `MemoryConversationStore`\n791:#### `DatabaseConversationStore`\n801:## Contract\n806:2. **`ProviderInterface` is the abstract inference boundary.** A provider turns a conversation (plus optional `tools`, a non-empty `ToolDefinition[]`) into a turn: `generate` resolves the assembled `ProviderResult`, `stream` yields channel-tagged `ProviderDelta`s and returns the assembled result. Both methods accept optional `ProviderStreamOptions`; `think` is the per-call reasoning override. It carries an `id` (a per-instance trace label) and `name` (the backend identifier). This module defines only the contract — a concrete implementation is a host application's responsibility.\n807:3. **`stream` yields deltas + returns the assembled result.** A `ProviderInterface.stream` yields each non-empty answer delta as `{ channel: 'content', text }` and each native live reasoning delta as `{ channel: 'thinking', text }`; its return value is the assembled `ProviderResult` whose `content` is the provider's authoritative clean answer, plus any tool calls and usage the turn reported. So a caller can render tokens and reasoning live while still recovering the complete outcome from the generator's return value. A thinking model's reasoning is separated at the provider, never assembled as content: a concrete provider routes its raw wire content through a `ThinkSplitter` (`createThinkSplitter` — one per stream), yields the clean content, assembles `content` from the splitter's authoritative accumulation (an implicit pre-seeded open — the qwen3-template bare `</think>` — reclassifies the already-yielded prefix into thinking, the one shape where live content deltas can transiently over-report), and surfaces the accumulated reasoning as `ProviderResult.thinking` — which never re-enters the conversation (the `Agent` joins it across a run's calls onto `AgentResult.thinking` as display/audit metadata).\n809:5. **Bounded by an `AbortSignal`.** Both `generate` and `stream` take an `AbortSignal`, so a caller bounds the request — a cancel, a [timeout](timeout.md), and a token [budget](budget.md) folded into one signal through `AbortSignal.any`. An already-aborted signal rejects the call before any content streams. (How a concrete provider also arms its own deadline is that implementation's own contract.)\n810:6. **Abort → `ProviderAbortError` with the partial.** A `stream` cancelled mid-flight throws a `ProviderAbortError` whose `partial` is the `ProviderResult` assembled from whatever streamed so far (content + any tool calls + any usage); `isProviderAbortError` narrows a caught value so the loop can recover the partial content. A non-abort error propagates unchanged. `ProviderAbortError` is the abstract boundary's error — it stays in this module so the agent loop catches it regardless of which backend is in use.\n814:10. **The agent loop (`Agent` / `createAgent`).** `Agent` composes a `ProviderInterface`, an `AgentContext`, and its `@orkestrel/tool` registry into the bounded context → provider → tools → repeat turn. It builds the provider input, then iterates up to `limit`: stream a provider turn, accumulate content/thinking/usage, append any assistant tool calls, and execute them. Each discriminated `ToolResult` becomes a tool message whose content is `JSON.stringify(result.value)` when `result.success` is true and `result.error` when false, so failure text is never JSON-quoted. A final assistant turn stops the loop.\n815:11. **One `#run` shared by `generate` + `stream`.** A single private async generator drives the whole turn. `stream()` exposes it as an `AgentStreamInterface` (`events` + `result` + `abort`); `generate()` drains that same stream (iterating `events`, discarding chunks) and returns its `result` — it has zero loop logic of its own, so `generate` and `stream` can never diverge (a `generate()` result deep-equals draining `stream()` on the same input).\n816:12. **The `AgentChunk` stream.** `stream().events` yields, per turn, each content delta as `{ category: 'token', content }`, each live reasoning delta as `{ category: 'think', content }`, then optional usage, then one `{ category: 'tool', call, result }` per dispatched call. The call and discriminated result use the contracts imported from `@orkestrel/tool`. `result` resolves the settled `AgentResult`: final or partial content, joined thinking, summed optional usage, and `partial`.\n817:13. **Bounded, paced, capped.** Each run arms one cancel through `createAbort({ signal: AbortSignal.any([…]) })` folding whichever of the external `signal`, the `timeout` deadline (a started `Timeout`), and the `budget` signal (a started `Budget`) are present; `agent.abort(reason)` / `stream.abort(reason)` fires it, and the timeout is always cleared in a `finally`. A cancel — external, deadline, budget, or `abort()` — stops the loop and the `result` promise resolves `{ partial: true, content: <accumulated> }`; it is not an error. The accumulated `content` already holds whatever streamed before the cancel — the loop accumulates each delta as it yields the `token` chunk, and a `ProviderAbortError.partial.content` is exactly those same yielded deltas (the `stream` contract), so the loop never re-adds it. A genuine provider / tool error (the signal is not aborted) rejects the `result` (and `status` → `error`). The optional `scheduler.yield({ signal })`s between turns (before turns 2…N, never after the last); tool iteration is capped at `limit` so the loop always terminates. `status` transitions `idle` → `running` → `done` / `error`.\n818:14. **Pull and push observation surfaces on the `Agent`; the rest event-free.** The provider contract, the tool registry, the conversation store, and the `AgentContext` itself carry no Emitter, no `EventMap`, no `on` hook — they stay purely functional (of the managers the context composes, the `InstructionManager` and `ScopeManager` carry their own `emitter`s and each `Conversation` owns one, while the `ToolManager`, `WorkspaceManager`, and `ConversationManager` are event-free — as is the context that composes them). The `Agent` itself carries both a pull and a push surface. Pull: the `AgentChunk` stream (`stream().events`) yields per-token answer deltas, per-think reasoning deltas, usage chunks, and tool chunks for a live consumer. Push: the `emitter` (`AgentEventMap`) — `start` (run begins), `turn` (each iteration), `tool` (a dispatched call + result), `usage` (a turn's usage), `deny` (an authority denial — not in the chunk stream), `finish` (the settled result), `error` (a genuine failure), `abort` (a cancel), `exhaust` (a limit exhausted with unresolved tool intent — fires instead of `abort`), and `fault` (a non-fatal automatic-compaction summarizer throw — the run continues; the automatic-compaction clause) — wired through the emitter pattern (`AgentOptions.on` hooks, the `AgentOptions.error` listener-error handler, a `readonly emitter`, `new Emitter({ on: options?.on, error: options?.error })`). Per-token / per-thinking deltas are the stream's job exclusively — there is deliberately no `token` or `think` event. The emitter isolates a listener throw (it can never escape into the settle-once / wake-park loop) and routes it to its own `error` handler (the `error` option, surfaced as `(error, event)`, not a domain event; itself guarded against re-entrancy), so observation is provably side-effect-free on the 3×-hardened loop — and every emit sits after the relevant state transition / settle, so it cannot reorder control flow. A cancelled run emits `abort` (the cancel reason) then `finish` (the settled partial); a genuine error emits `error` instead of `finish`. The loop's deterministic logic is pinned in the `src:core` mirror with a scripted `ProviderInterface`.\n819:15. **Doc ↔ source method bijection.** The `## Methods` tables list exactly the public methods of every agent-owned interface named there, exhaustive in both directions, and each agent-owned concrete class exposes the same public methods as its interface. `MessageManagerInterface` is implemented structurally by the active `Conversation`; `ProviderInterface` is implemented by the host. Dependency method surfaces belong to [`tool.md`](tool.md) and [`workspace.md`](workspace.md).\n823:19. **Partial = configurable failure (`partial`).** An `Agent.generate()` resolves `partial: true` on a cancel (abort / budget / timeout) — a cancel is not an error. For a durable job that is, by default, a failure: the `createAgentQueue` / `createAgentRunner` handler throws an `AgentJobError` (carrying the partial `AgentResult`), so the Queue's retries re-run the job and a Runner's fail-fast aborts its siblings. Pass `partial: true` to treat a partial as success instead — the handler resolves the partial result rather than throwing. `isAgentJobError` narrows a caught value to recover its `partial`.\n825:21. **Sub-agent fan-out through `controller.spawn`; cancellation threaded.** On a `createAgentRunner`, each unit's handler receives a `ControllerInterface`; before running the (parent) job it `controller.spawn`s each of the job's declared `children` (fire-and-track, never inline-awaited — a slot-holding bounded handler awaiting its own spawn can deadlock), so each child is a real sub-agent run through the same bounded queue whose result joins the run after the declared jobs (in spawn order). `createAgentQueue` ignores `children` (a queue has no fan-out). Cancellation threads through in both: the handler passes `context.signal` (queue) / `controller.signal` (runner) into `registry.build`, so a queue / runner `abort()` or a per-attempt timeout fires the rehydrated agent's signal (which commits a partial — the `partial` policy then decides). All event-free.\n828:24. **Automatic compaction — the context `window` budget (opt-in, additive, production-hardened).** `AgentOptions.window` is a context [`Budget`](budget.md) (`BudgetInterface<readonly Message[]>`) for automatic conversation compaction, enabled only when both a `window` budget is set and the active conversation is `summarizable` (it has a summarizer — the conversation-layer clause). There is always an active conversation (the active-conversation clause), but the default one has no summarizer, so this `summarizable` gate preserves the shipped behavior: a non-summarizable conversation is never auto-compacted, and the auto path never throws the `compact()` `SUMMARIZER` error. Its `consumer` is a pluggable token estimator (for example the exported `estimateMessages`) and its `max` is the context window — the same consume-to-a-ceiling primitive as the cost `budget` (the bounded-paced-capped clause), but its ceiling action is compact instead of abort. The loop's private `#trim` runs the check at these points: (1) before the first provider request (so a resumed / already-long conversation whose initial prompt already exceeds the window compacts at once, not only after a tool turn), and (2) between turns on the tool-iteration `continue` path (after the prior turn's `usage` was folded and its assistant + tool messages were appended, before the next provider request; never after the final assistant turn that ends the loop). The `window` budget is reset (`clear()`) at run entry, so no stale `consumed` carries across runs or a conversation switch. Each check measures the absolute current prompt: it `clear()`s the budget then `consume`s the working message array — the exact next prompt (the system block + the active conversation's `view()` + this turn's appended messages, that is, what the next `provider.stream` will receive) — so `window.consumed` is the current full prompt's estimated footprint and `window.exhausted` means that prompt has reached the context window `max`. When the prompt `exhausted`s the window, `#trim` `await`s `conversation.compact()` (folding the older live tail into a summarized `Section` through the conversation's own `ConversationSummaryHandler`), then rebuilds the working message array from `context.build(provider.format)` — the same projection the loop opened with — so the run continues on the (now smaller) compacted context. No post-compact `clear()` is needed: the next check's `clear()` + `consume` re-measures the now-shrunken prompt. This is distinct from the hard `budget` ceiling (the bounded-paced-capped clause), which aborts the run with a partial. **Production hardening:** (a) non-fatal summarizer failure — the automatic `compact()` is wrapped in try/catch: a thrown summarizer error does not crash the run; the loop skips compaction that turn, surfaces the error as a `fault` event (so it is observable, never silently lost), and continues (the over-window prompt proceeds to the provider). Only the auto path is resilient — a manual `conversation.compact()` still propagates its error. (b) futile-compaction guard (the single-level limit) — when `compact()` resolves `undefined` (nothing left to fold) while the prompt is still over the window (the section summaries alone exceed it), a per-run flag latches so auto-compaction stops for the rest of that run (no per-turn churn); the over-window prompt then proceeds to the provider, which surfaces a genuine context-length error if it truly cannot fit (the real limit) — the loop does not loop futilely. The whole path is opt-in: with no `window` budget, or a non-summarizable active conversation, `#trim` (the run-entry reset, the pre-first-turn check, the between-turns check) is skipped entirely, adding no `await` before the first provider request, so a cost-budget-only agent's eager-pump and abort timing are untouched. Observability is the conversation's own `compact` / `summary` events (the conversation-layer clause) plus the agent's `fault` (the `strict` clause). These limits are deliberate: the automatic summarizer call is the conversation's configured one and is not separately bound to the run's abort signal; and compaction is single-level, so a conversation whose section summaries alone exceed `window` cannot shrink further — the futile guard stops the churn rather than pretending otherwise. The deterministic behavior (the absolute prompt crossing `max`, the fold, the rebuilt-smaller prompt, the pre-first-turn fold, the non-fatal `fault` path, the futile guard, no-fire below the ceiling) is pinned in the `src:core` mirror with a scripted provider, a data-stub (and a throwing) summarizer, and the real `estimateMessages` estimator, including a run forced through two or more folds that stays coherent.\n834:27. **Limit exhaustion, mid-stream budget metering, per-run bounds, and per-run `schema`.** `RunOutcome.exhausted` (and the settled outcome's `partial`) flips `true` when the turn loop exhausts the effective `limit` while the most recently completed turn still held unresolved tool intent (the model requested tools on the very last allowed turn) — a cause distinct from a cancel: it fires the `exhaust` event (carrying the effective `limit`) instead of `abort`, still followed by `finish` carrying the partial result. A natural final answer on the last allowed turn, or `limit: 0` (which never enters the loop), stays `partial: false` with no `exhaust`. **Mid-stream budget charging.** During each provider turn, `#provide`'s `onDelta` re-estimates the turn's accumulated content through `estimateTokens` (`ceil(length / 4)`) and `budget.consume`s only the increment over what was already charged this turn (`{ prompt: 0, completion: increment, total: increment }`) — so the budget trip can land mid-stream, before the turn's final `usage` is known; the tripped signal folds into the run's bound abort exactly like any other cancel (the run resolves `partial: true` with an `abort` event, the provider genuinely cancelled). Once the turn's `usage` is known, a residual reconcile charges the remainder (`{ prompt: usage.prompt, completion: max(0, usage.completion - charged), total: max(0, usage.total - charged) }`), so the turn's total budget draw nets to exactly the authoritative usage — never double-charged, never lost. The reported `AgentResult.usage` / `usage` chunks are always the full authoritative usage, unaffected by how the budget was charged. **Per-run overrides (`AgentRunOptions`).** `limit` / `timeout` / `budget` / `signal` each override their `AgentOptions` construction default for this run only (`??` semantics — an omitted key keeps the constructed default); a per-run `signal` composes with (never replaces) a constructed `signal` through `AbortSignal.any` — either aborting cancels the run; a per-run `budget` is `start()`ed for that run and is the one the loop charges, leaving a constructed `budget` untouched for that run. **Per-run `schema`.** `AgentRunOptions.schema` (and `ProviderStreamOptions.schema`) mirrors `think` — a per-run structured-output constraint forwarded to `provider.stream`. `#provide` composes `think` and `schema` into one options object, omitting whichever key is `undefined`, and passes no options object at all when both are absent.\n839:31. **`sanitizeUsage` — normalizing a provider's abort-time partial usage.** A `ProviderAbortError.partial.usage` a provider surfaces mid-abort can be malformed (non-finite, negative, or fractional fields) since it was assembled from a cut-off stream rather than a clean settle. `sanitizeToken(value)` is the shared per-field primitive: it floors non-finite or non-positive values to `0` and positive fractional values to their integer part. `sanitizeUsage(usage)` applies it to `prompt` / `completion` / `total`, so a caller charging a token `Budget` never consumes a negative or fractional amount. The `Agent` loop applies it automatically to an abort's partial usage before folding it into the run's accounted usage / budget charge; both helpers are also exported standalone.\n842:33. **`agentResultToJSON` — the canonical portable AgentResult projection.** The helper accepts `unknown` and never throws, including for throwing getters, hostile nested usage, and revoked proxies. It captures `content`, `thinking`, `usage`, and `partial` exactly once through Contract's sanctioned `attempt` boundary, so conforming accessors and inherited structural properties are supported without a second read. `content` must be a string and `partial` a boolean; absent/`undefined` `thinking` and `usage` are omitted, while present `thinking` must be a string and present `usage` an object whose `prompt` / `completion` / `total` fields are finite numbers. Finite negative and fractional counts are preserved rather than normalized through `sanitizeUsage`, because the authoritative `TokenUsage` contract is numeric. Extra input fields are dropped. The helper rebuilds a fresh exact plain `{ content, thinking?, usage?: { prompt, completion, total }, partial }` object, deep-gates it through Contract's `parseJSONValue`, and returns Contract's imported `JSONValue`; invalid input returns `undefined`.\n844:## Patterns\n846:### Bounding any provider call\n848:`ProviderInterface.generate` / `.stream` take a plain `AbortSignal`, so fold an [abort](abort.md), a [timeout](timeout.md), and a token [budget](budget.md) into one bound through `AbortSignal.any` — whichever trips first cancels the call. This works for any provider; constructing the concrete one is a host application's job.\n851:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n852:import { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\n856:declare const provider: ProviderInterface // any concrete implementation supplied by the host app\n857:const abort = createAbort() // external cancel\n863:const bound = AbortSignal.any([abort.signal, timeout.signal, budget.signal])\n868:### Dispatching the model's tool calls\n873:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n876:declare const provider: ProviderInterface\n887:### Running the loop (instead of driving the provider by hand)\n893:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n895:declare const provider: ProviderInterface\n900:setTimeout(() => agent.abort('user navigated away'), 5_000)\n906:### Bounding cost mid-stream (the token `budget`)\n908:An `AgentOptions.budget` (or a per-run override) is not only charged from each turn's final reported `usage` — the loop also charges it incrementally, mid-stream, from an estimated token count as content deltas arrive (the `estimateTokens` `ceil(length / 4)` heuristic), so a runaway completion trips the ceiling without waiting for the turn to finish. When the mid-stream estimate crosses the budget, the budget's `signal` fires, folding into the run's bound abort exactly like an external cancel or a `timeout` — the provider is cancelled, and the run resolves `partial: true` with an `abort` event (the same funnel as any other cancel).\n918:agent.emitter.on('abort', (reason) => log('budget tripped mid-stream', reason))\n923:**A `think: true` run needs headroom for reasoning.** Live reasoning deltas (`ProviderDelta` `'thinking'`) are not metered mid-stream (only `'content'` deltas are — thinking is charged, like content, solely through the post-turn reconcile), so a thinking model can spend a large share of a tight budget's ceiling on its reasoning before any answer content streams — the mid-stream trip can land while the model is still reasoning, committing an empty (or near-empty) `content` alongside `partial: true`. Give a `think: true` run enough budget headroom to cover its reasoning, not only its expected answer length.\n925:### Observing an agent (push vs. pull)\n927:An `Agent` exposes a pull and a push observation surface. Pull — the `AgentChunk` stream (`stream().events`) — is for a live consumer rendering per-token answer deltas and per-think reasoning deltas as they arrive. Push — the `emitter` (`AgentEventMap`) — is for fire-and-forget observers (logging, metrics, tracing) that want the loop's lifecycle moments without draining the stream: `start` (a run begins), `turn` (each iteration), `tool` (a dispatched call + its result), `usage` (a turn's token usage), `deny` (an authority denial — which never reaches the chunk stream), `finish` (the settled result), `error` (a genuine failure), `abort` (a cancel), and `exhaust` (the limit was reached while the model still held unresolved tool intent — fires instead of `abort`, still followed by `finish`). Per-token / per-thinking deltas stay the stream's job exclusively — there is deliberately no `token` or `think` event on the emitter; reach for the stream when you need live output, the emitter when you need lifecycle.\n931:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n933:declare const provider: ProviderInterface\n944:agent.emitter.on('abort', (reason) => log('cancelled', reason))\n949:**A cancelled run emits `abort` then `finish`.** A cancel (an external `signal`, the `timeout` deadline, an exhausted `budget`, or `abort()`) still resolves a partial result — so the emitter fires `abort` (carrying the cancel reason) and then `finish` (carrying the settled partial), letting an observer see both that the run was cancelled and the partial outcome it committed. A natural / cap-bounded finish fires `finish` only; a genuine provider / tool error fires `error` instead of `finish`. `generate()` and `stream()` drive the same events (they share one `#run`).\n951:### Pulling context from another conversation (with provenance)\n959:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n961:declare const provider: ProviderInterface\n986:### Persisting a conversation through either store\n1015:### Running many durable agents as jobs\n1054:### Giving the model documents to read\n1060:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n1063:declare const provider: ProviderInterface\n1074:### Switching which workspace the model sees\n1080:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n1082:declare const provider: ProviderInterface\n1101:### Removing / clearing entries, and the less-common accessors\n1116:import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\n1118:declare const provider: ProviderInterface\n1171:### Practices\n1173:- **Bound every call** — pass an `AbortSignal` (an [abort](abort.md), or an `AbortSignal.any` over abort + [timeout](timeout.md) + [budget](budget.md)) so a request can be cancelled, deadlined, or capped.\n1183:- **Pull and push surfaces on the `Agent`, none elsewhere** — observe the `Agent` each way: pull the `AgentChunk` stream for per-token / per-thinking deltas + usage/tool chunks, or push `agent.emitter.on(...)` (`AgentEventMap`) for lifecycle + usage/tool/deny moments a fire-and-forget observer wants. A listener throw can never corrupt the loop (the emitter isolates it, routing it to the `error` option). Do not reach for an Emitter on the provider contract, the tool registry, the conversation store, the context, or the job layer — those stay event-free; and do not expect per-token `token` or `think` events (those stay the stream's job).\n1187:## Tests\n1194:- [`tests/src/core/helpers.test.ts`](../tests/src/core/helpers.test.ts) — agent-owned pure helpers: `agentResultToJSON` full/minimal fresh exact projections + extras dropped + compile-time exhaustive `AgentResult` field-map review, conforming accessor/inherited structural values, and total rejection of wrong/missing fields, malformed present optionals, non-finite/missing usage counts, throwing getters, non-object inputs, and revoked root/nested proxies; `filterAllowList`'s semantics (`undefined` ⇒ all, `[]` ⇒ none, a list ⇒ only-listed) preserving item order (not allow-list order), ignoring unknown keys, matching through the key extractor (not identity), and returning `[]` (not throwing) for an empty item list; `estimateTokens` / `estimateMessages` (the per-message `MESSAGE_TOKEN_OVERHEAD`, an empty batch ⇒ 0, empty content ⇒ overhead only, the JSON-stringified `calls` contribution with its documented fixed fallback on a circular argument and no contribution for an empty `calls` array, and `images.length * IMAGE_TOKEN_ESTIMATE` per image); `renderFencedFile` (the `File:` label + language-tagged fence, the body verbatim across lines, and a workspace text file framed from its own text arm); `sanitizeToken` / `sanitizeUsage` (identity on a well-formed non-negative integer usage; `NaN` / negative / `±Infinity` floored to 0 and a fractional field to its integer part, each field independently); and `settleAgentJob`'s partial policy (a natural finish resolves `partial: false`, a disallowed partial throws an `AgentJobError` carrying the partial, an allowed partial resolves as success). And the extracted loop / cascade / conversation / scope leaves on their own contracts: `joinThinking` / `sumUsage` seeding then accumulating, `assembleResult` omitting an absent `thinking` / `usage` and keeping the loop-internal `exhausted` out of the public result, `denyCall`'s denial texts, `renderSection` rendering nothing for an empty item list, `resolveOpen` / `resolveClose` / `resolveItem` at each cascade level (item override beating every other), `attachImages` merging own-images-first without mutating the source, `attachUserImages` replacing only the last user turn (and returning the conversation unchanged for no data or no user turn), `collectImageData` skipping a text file, `buildSummaryMessage` / `buildRecapMessage` (raw vs. `CONVERSATION_RECAP_PREFIX`-framed), and `intersectKeys` treating `undefined` as the universal set and returning a copy.\n1195:- [`tests/src/core/factories.test.ts`](../tests/src/core/factories.test.ts) — agent-owned factories over real declared tool/workspace dependencies: `createAgentContext` (the `[system?, ...messages]` assembly; One splitter serves one stream; create a fresh\r\n *   one per call ({@link import('./factories.js').createThinkSplitter}).\r\n */\r\nexport interface ThinkSplitterInterface {\r\n\t/** Holds the authoritative clean content accumulated so far (corrected across an implicit-open reclassification). */\r\n\treadonly content: string\r\n\t/** Holds the reasoning text accumulated from every `<think>�?�</think>` span so far. */\r\n\treadonly thinking: string\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Feeds one raw delta and returns the clean, non-think content to surface for it (possibly\r\n\t * `''`) �?\" a tag split across deltas is held until disambiguated, never leaked as content\r\n\t * and never mis-eaten as thinking.\r\n\t */\r\n\tsplit(delta: string): string\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Settles the stream end �?\" a held partial tag that never completed returns as the final\r\n\t * content delta, and an unclosed think span's tail lands on `thinking`.\r\n\t */\r\n\tflush(): string\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Stores immutable {@link Message}s in insertion order and mints each `id` on `add` �?\" the\r\n * message-store contract {@link AgentContextInterface.messages} is typed to, which the active\r\n * {@link ConversationInterface} satisfies structurally.\r\nimport type {\r\n\tAgentResult,\r\n\tProviderErrorCode,\r\n\tProviderErrorOptions,\r\n\tProviderResult,\r\n} from './types.js'\r\n\r\n// A real error type, not a sentinel. `stream` throws a\r\n// ProviderAbortError when its bound signal aborts mid-flight, carrying the partial\r\n// result it had assembled so far so the agent loop can recover the streamed content\r\n// on cancellation. Strike a unit whose subject a later unit deletes. State the new\r\n     prerequisite of a unit that acquired one.\r\n   - Walk the remaining units once and ask of each whether what landed still supports it. A\r\n     decision taken inside a unit can remove a later unit's foundation, and the unit that took it\r\n     cannot see that.\r\n   - Re-baseline when a probe overturns a decision the plan rests on, not only at a phase boundary.\r\n     A measurement that falsifies your own reconciliation changes which units run.\r\n   - Record what changed and why. An unrecorded re-baseline cannot be audited, and the next one\r\n     re-derives it.\r\n8. **Accept.** Decide, then report outcomes, decisions, evidence, and remaining risk concisely.\r\n   When the design step's exit criterion is met and the gates are green, accept. The next goal is the\r\n   deliverable.\r\n\r\n### Re-baselining is not rescoping\r\n\r\n`.claude/rules/quality.md` fixes the enumerated scope when work begins and forbids reopening it. A\r\nre-baseline changes which units run. It never changes the goal's exit criterion.\r\n\r\nStrike a unit because the phase satisfied it, never because it became inconvenient. Add a unit\r\nbecause implementation revealed work the exit criterion already required, never because an engine\r\nthought of something else worth doing. A re-baseline that moves the exit criterion is a rescope, and\r\nthat needs the user.\r\n\r\n## Deviation protocol\r\n\r\nWhen reality diverges from a writing dispatch:\r\n\r\n1. The writer stops and reports: expected, found, exact evidence, done or not done, and at most one\r\n   short hypothesis. It does not investigate, improvise, or alter the plan.\r\n2. The Orchestrator triages:\r\n   - obvious correction �+' tighten and re-dispatch;\r\n   - missing mechanical evidence �+' dispatch `verifier`;\r\n   - unknown terrain �+' dispatch `grok` with the report and the plan slice;\r\n   - unknown design or root cause �+' dispatch `planner` and `analyst` on the question.\r\n3. The Orchestrator decides, updates the plan, and re-dispatches.\r\n\r\nWorkflow failures use the same ladder. Do not absorb their raw logs into the main context.\r\n\r\n## Dispatch anatomy\r\n\r\n### Native first\r\n\r\nLaunch a model through the running harness's own mechanism whenever that harness hosts it: Claude\r\nsubagents and workflows in Claude Code, Codex-native agents in a Codex session, Cursor-native\r\nsessions in Cursor. Serialize writing\r\n  nodes; never run concurrent writers in the tree.\r\n- Name a role and its engine in every node.\r\n\r\nThe harness bridge names the concrete mechanism for each of these.\r\n\r\n### Every dispatch is a file before it is a launch\r\n\r\n- Write the brief to a file under `tmp/`, named for its unit, before launching the unit, whatever\r\n  engine executes it. A brief composed only inside a launch argument cannot be corrected, resumed,\r\n  or re-run after that call ends. A native unit's pair is `tmp/units/<unit>-brief.md` and\r\n  `tmp/units/<unit>-report.md`. A bench unit's pair sits under the bench directory its role file\r\n  names, beside the journal.\r\n- Write the unit's returned report in the SAME action that commits its code, never afterwards. A\r\n  commit message states what changed; the report states what the unit measured, what it decided, what\r\n  it could not close, and which of its own claims it flagged. An auditor's subject is the report,\r\n  so a report living only in the Orchestrator's context stops the next lane on arrival.\r\n- Capture the unit's returned report to a file beside its brief under the same unit name, so a\r\n  unit's instruction and its outcome are one pair on disk.\r\n- Name, inside a report that rests on a bench lane, that lane's journal path and session id, so\r\n  the provenance survives the journal's sweep.\r\n- Amend a brief on re-run rather than restating it. Every law here binds all of them.\r\n\r\n### Launching\r\n\r\n- The Orchestrator launches every long command as a harness-tracked background command under a hard\r\n  time cap. Never detach one from inside a dispatched agent. The harness owns the lifecycle,\r\n  completion re-invokes the session, and the cap kills a wedged command loudly instead of trusting\r\n  the agent to report its own failure. A wedged bridge is silent, and silence must never read as\r\n  progress.\r\n- Never replicate git commits through a hosting provider's REST API when a file's content must ride\r\n  inside the tool-call JSON. The per-call read cap truncates it, and a lockfile is the file that\r\n  proves the point: it transits incomplete and the tree it lands in installs something else. Push\r\n  over git, or move the file another way.\r\n- Write a multi-step chain to a script file and run the file. A chain composed inside one shell\r\n  argument cannot be read back, corrected, or re-run, and the record of what actually ran is the\r\n  argument text in a transcript rather than a file on disk.\r\n- Never edit a script file while a shell is executing it. `bash` reads a script incrementally from a\r\n  byte offset rather than loading it, so an edit that shifts line numbers moves the text under that\r\n  offset and the shell resumes mid-construct. The run dies on a syntax error in a line the script\r\n  does not contain, which reads as a defect in the work rather than as the edit that caused it. Copy\r\n  the file, edit the copy, and launch the copy for the next run. A successor's header names its own\r\n  file, its own log, and what changed from the file it supersedes.\r\n- On a Windows host this binds every program-carrying command, not only long ones. Heredocs,\r\n  `node -e`, `node -p`, `&&` chaining, and any argument carrying `${...}` trip the Git Bash\r\n  approval classifier and turn an unattended run into a manual approval prompt. Write the program\r\n  to a file, invoke the file, and keep each shell call one plain command.\r\n- Detach anything that must survive its launching shell with `setsid`. A backgrounded flow the\r\n  harness reaps mid-step leaves the work half done and the exit status missing, and the reap looks\r\n  identical to the step failing.\r\n- Size the cap yourself, from the observed high mark of comparable commands, plus an\r\n  independently budgeted gate allowance, plus explicit slack. Never size it from the estimate\r\n  alone. Never delegate it: a bridge starts with a clean context, holds no record of prior runs,\r\n  and can only guess. A cap-killed exec is indistinguishable from a real failure.\r\n- Run the first use of any CLI flag, subcommand, quoting form, or stdin combination in a throwaway\r\n  probe. Never inside a dispatched unit or a publish chain.\r\n- A launch is not a launch until its record grows past its header. Confirm the log advanced beyond\r\n  the head before recording that the command started, and treat an instantly-dead log as a failed\r\n  launch whose tail is the evidence.\r\n- Keep network-dependent work out of sandboxed bench execs. Bench sandboxes deny network, so\r\n  lockfile generation, real installs, and live fetches belong to the Orchestrator's own tracked\r\n  commands or to the native `implementer` or `builder` as an ordinary dispatched writing unit. A\r\n  bench exec hanging on `npm` until its cap fires is the signature of this misroute, not of a slow\r\n  bench.\r\n- A Workflow journals identically and dies identically, so give it the same watch �?\" with one\r\n  correction. A workflow journal writes only at agent start and result, so its mtime goes quiet for\r\n  minutes during healthy work, and the liveness signal is the newest subagent transcript instead. A\r\n  watch that reports only new events cannot report a death, because silence and progress look the\r\n  same; the filter must fire on absence. Recover with `resumeFromRunId`, which returns every\r\n  completed agent from cache and re-runs only what never finished.\r\n\r\n### Reading liveness\r\n\r\nRead liveness from the artifact the work produces, never from its wrapper. A subagent's transcript\r\nfile can report zero bytes while the agent is working normally, so an empty or stale wrapper proves\r\nnothing.\r\n\r\n- Judge a unit by what it has changed in the tree: modification times on the files it owns, the\r\n  counts its suite reports, the report it was told to write.\r\n- Check that before killing anything. A healthy unit killed on a false signal loses everything it\r\n  had not yet written down, and the loss is charged to the orchestrator, not the unit.\r\n- If a unit must be stopped, say plainly that it was stopped and why, then assess the tree it left\r\n  rather than assuming its partial bytes are either good or worthless.\r\n- Follow the deviation ladder for a stalled journal or a cap-killed exec, using the session id from\r\n  the journal head as the recovery handle.\r\n\r\n### Confirm dead before relaunching\r\n\r\n- Prove the previous run is gone before starting another. List the processes and read the list. A\r\n  second run started beside a live first one produces failures that read as the subject's �?\" a\r\n  publish chain relaunched over a live one reports `EOTP` and `E403` that are its own processes\r\n  colliding, and both readings point at the registry.\r\n- Never ask `pgrep -f` or `ps | grep` whether a command is running from a shell whose own command line\r\n  contains that command's text. The shell matches itself, so the answer is yes whatever the truth is.\r\n  This bites in separate places and each one fails differently:\r\n  - **A liveness watcher** loops forever, reporting \"still running\" and never delivering its completion\r\n    notification, because it always finds itself.\r\n  - **An elapsed-time reading** returns the watcher's age rather than the exec's, so the number falls\r\n    instead of rising and reads as a relaunch that never happened.\r\n  - **A pre-launch \"is anything already running\" check** reports a phantom concurrent writer, which is\r\n    the worst of them: the honest response to it is to kill something, and there is nothing there.\r\n    Read liveness from the recorded process id with `kill -0 <pid>`, or enumerate by executable name and\r\n    parent with `ps -eo pid,ppid,comm` and read the rows. Both are immune; a pattern over the full command\r\n    line is not.\r\n- Kill by process id, never by pattern. `pkill -f` matches the relaunch that is already starting, so\r\n  the pattern that cleans up the old run kills the new one and the cleanup reads as a launch\r\n  failure.\r\n- A killed `codex exec` is dead only when its process tree is dead: walk the children with\r\n  `ps --ppid` and confirm the `codex-code-mode-host` child is gone. Before dispatching a substitute\r\n  writer, check the owned files' modification times against the baseline �?\" a live orphan is still\r\n  writing the tree the substitute is about to own.\r\n- Read a failure against what was running when it happened, not against what you believe was\r\n  running. Exit the filter on the exec's terminal event so no\r\n   watcher outlives its subject. The journal's mtime is the liveness signal; the session id in its\r\n   head is the recovery handle. The journal is also the proof the bench ran: a bench unit returns\r\n   its journal path and session id with its result, and the Orchestrator confirms both before\r\n   using that result.
